<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting nonnegative integer matrices with prescribed row sums, column sums, and individual entry bounds. Both dimensions vary, all numerical data are encoded in binary, and zero bounds are allowed. The algorithm uses only unbiased random bits and has a polynomial bound on its bit operations on every execution.

- **claim-main-fpras** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is one randomized algorithm with the following guarantee. Given $(r,c,b)$ as above and rational $\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)$, it outputs a nonnegative rational $\widehat Z$ such that $$\mathbb P\bigl((1-\varepsilon)Z(r,c,b)\le \widehat Z \le(1+\varepsilon)Z(r,c,b)\bigr)\ge1-\delta.$$ If $\Omega(r,c,b)$ is empty, the output is zero on every execution. The algorithm uses independent unbiased bits, and one fixed polynomial in $L$, $\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\log(\delta^{-1})$ bounds its total bit operations on every execution, where $L$ is the total binary input length, including the accuracy parameters. No counting or sampling oracle is required.
  - Explanation: A single randomized procedure estimates the number of cell-bounded tables within the requested relative tolerance and failure probability. Its bit cost is bounded by a fixed polynomial on every execution, and an empty table set always yields zero.
- **claim-forest-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forest)): For a free cell $e$, every tree cell $t$ on the path joining its endpoints satisfies $b_e\le b_t$. For every box-valid integer small profile $(x,y)$ and every real free vector $z\in\prod_{e\in\mathcal F}[0,b_e+1]$, the nonroot equations have a unique solution. This solution is integral for integral $z$, and the root deviations are independent of $z$.* *For any fixed feasible table $X^*$ of the tightened instance, $$\label{eq:forest-displacement} \|z_t-X^*_t\|\le4db_t\quad(t\in\mathcal T), \qquad \sum_{v\text{ a root}}\|\delta_v\|\le2dW.$$ Consequently, throughout the extended free box, $$0\le D\le H,\qquad z_t\in[-10db_t,10db_t]\quad(t\in\mathcal T).$$ For fixed $(x,y)$, set $u_e=z_e/(b_e+1)$. As a function of $u$, the penalty $D$ is convex on $[0,1]^{\mathcal F}$ and satisfies $$\label{eq:forest-lipschitz} \|D(x,y,u)-D(x,y,u')\| \le2Ad^2\\|u-u'\\|_\infty.$$ For an empty free set the domain is a singleton and the last bound has its usual vacuous interpretation.
  - Explanation: Free cells never exceed the capacities of tree cells on their cycle path. The tree values are then uniquely and integrally determined by leaf elimination, and the root deviations do not depend on the free values. This yields displacement bounds, penalty bounds and a Lipschitz estimate.
- **claim-contamination-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-contamination)): For the feasible tightened instance, $$Z\le C(H)\le(1+\varepsilon/32)Z.$$
  - Explanation: The softened count at the final annealing index sandwiches the true number of feasible tables, with only a small relative excess caused by invalid completions.
- **claim-integer-signature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-integer-signature)): Suppose $M_t\ge1$ for every pair that is summed. For every box-valid display $z$ on the remaining coordinates with $\sum_i z_i=r+2$, the symmetric matrix $$\bigl(\Phi(z-e_i-e_h)\bigr)_{i,h} \quad\text{has at most one positive eigenvalue}. \label{eq:integer-removal-signature}$$ The diagonal entries in this matrix are the weights after two removals from the same coordinate; invalid removals have weight zero. The corresponding assertion for two additions holds for every box-valid display of total $r-2$.
  - Explanation: Summing laminar log-concave weights over balanced coordinate pairs, including pairs of width one, keeps the two-removal and two-addition matrices at most one positive eigenvalue.
- **claim-hj-signatures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-hj-signatures)): For $0\le j\le H$, the matrix of two-removal weights of $h_j$ at every box-valid display of total $p+2$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. The corresponding two-addition matrix at every box-valid display of total $p-2$ has the same property.
  - Explanation: The softened completion weights at every annealing index inherit the one-positive-eigenvalue property for both removals and additions, via a laminar model and balanced summation.
- **claim-tree-marginal-logconcave** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-marginal)): For integers $v<k<w$ such that $a_t(v),a_t(w)>0$, $$a_t(k)\ge a_t(v)^{(w-k)/(w-v)} a_t(w)^{(k-v)/(w-v)}. \label{eq:tree-marginal-interpolation}$$ Consequently the positive support of $a_t$ is an integer interval.
  - Explanation: The marginal mass of a tree-cell value under the final softened sum is log-concave across the integers, so its positive support has no internal gap.
- **claim-binary-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-contraction)): The addition property, with positivity on the entire valid-degree domain, is preserved by fixing elements included or excluded and by summing a two-element group at occupancy one. The same assertions hold for the omission property. In addition, the cubic contraction used to sum a pair has at most one positive eigenvalue on its full remaining index set, before the contracted pair’s indices are removed.
  - Explanation: The addition and omission properties survive fixing included or excluded elements and summing a two-element group at occupancy one; a cubic tensor argument also controls the full matrix before the contracted indices are dropped.
- **claim-binary-signatures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-signatures)): The weight $f_j$ has the omission property, and $\widetilde f_j$ has the addition property. These properties and positivity remain valid after fixing any included or excluded elements and summing any disjoint labeled pairs at occupancy one, whenever the residual degree is valid. All assertions concern the full valid-degree subset domain.
  - Explanation: The first factorial lift has the omission property and the second has the addition property, on the full valid-degree domain and after the closure operations.
- **claim-distinct-lifts-example** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-rem-distinct-lifts)): Even with a constant count weight, the factorial construction need not give $f$ the addition property. For example, take two small cells with bounds $3$ and $2$, with base weight $h\equiv1$, so the binary ground set has ten elements and $p=5$. Fix as included the three row elements of the first cell. The seven remaining elements are its three column elements and the four elements of the second cell. In the addition matrix of $f$, restrict to vectors with common value $a$ on the first group of three and common value $b$ on the second group of four. Direct substitution in <a href="#eq:lifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lifts">[eq:lifts]</a> gives the quadratic form $$6a^2+12ab+8b^2.$$ Its coefficient matrix $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}6&6\\6&8\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ is positive definite, with determinant $12$. Thus this addition matrix has at least two positive eigenvalues. The omission property of $f$ and the addition property of $\widetilde f$ are the respective assertions needed below.
  - Explanation: An explicit small example shows the first lift does not generally satisfy the addition property, so the two signature roles must be kept apart.
- **claim-defect-inequalities** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-defect-inequalities)): For every partial assignment $\sigma$ and distinct unassigned labels $i,l$, $$4\widetilde c_{il}^{\sigma}\widetilde c_{li}^{\sigma} \le z_\sigma^2. \label{eq:defect-two-pair}$$ For three distinct unassigned labels $i,l,k$, $$\widetilde c_{il}^{\sigma}\widetilde c_{lk}^{\sigma} \le z_\sigma\widetilde c_{ik}^{\sigma}. \label{eq:defect-three-pair}$$
  - Explanation: Pair and triple inequalities for conditional defect masses follow from the addition property of the second lift via a two-by-two determinant and a Schur complement argument.
- **claim-one-hole-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport)): Fix disjoint sets of included and excluded elements. Let $F$ be the set of fixed inclusions, and partition the remaining elements into two singleton slots $P_0,P_1$ and $s$ ordinary slots of two elements each. Assume $\|F\|+s+1=p$. Let $\mathcal X_{\mathrm{sl}}$ consist of the sets that respect the fixed assignments and select one element from every slot except one hole slot, from which they select nothing. Give a state with hole slot $i$ weight $W_i f(S)$, where every $W_i$ is positive. For a real function $g$ on $\mathcal X_{\mathrm{sl}}$, let $D_{\mathrm{sl}}(g)$ be the unordered-edge energy on its single-exchange graph, with conductance equal to the minimum of the two endpoint weights.* *Let $s_0,s_1$ be the unmultiplied $f$-masses of the states with hole $P_0,P_1$, and let $\bar g_0,\bar g_1$ be their respective $f$-weighted means. For each ordinary slot $t$, let $v_t$ be the unmultiplied mass of sets that omit both singleton slots, select both elements of $t$, and select one element from every other ordinary slot, while respecting the fixed assignments. Then $$(\bar g_0-\bar g_1)^2 \leq \left( \frac{1}{s_0W_{P_0}}+\frac{1}{s_1W_{P_1}} +\frac{2}{s_0s_1}\sum_t\frac{v_t}{W_t} \right)D_{\mathrm{sl}}(g). \label{eq:slot-transport}$$ The notation $W_i$ here denotes slot multipliers, independently of the capacity threshold $W$.
  - Explanation: A signed flow between two one-hole classes has energy controlled by recursive potentials, using the omission property to bound each increment. This bounds the squared difference of weighted means by the Dirichlet energy.
- **claim-transversal-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-transversal-variance)): Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>. For every real function $g$ on $\mathcal X$, $$C\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu(g)\leq C_{\mathrm p}\mathfrak D(g). \label{eq:transversal-variance}$$
  - Explanation: For good multipliers, the variance of any observable restricted to transversals is bounded by an explicit constant times the Dirichlet form of the enlarged chain.
- **claim-defect-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-defect-mean)): Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>, and let $g$ be a real function on $\mathcal X$. For each defect type $il$ there is a conditional transversal mean $\bar g'$ such that $$(\bar g_{il}-\bar g')^2 \leq \frac{C_{\mathrm d}}{C}\mathfrak D(g), \qquad (\bar g'-\mathbb E_\mu g)^2\leq4\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu(g). \label{eq:defect-mean-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: The mean of an observable on any defect type lies close to a conditional transversal mean, which in turn lies close to the transversal mean, with explicit constants.
- **claim-observable-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-observable-variance)): Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>. For every real $g$ on $\mathcal X$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\pi(\mathcal Qg) \leq(9C_{\mathrm p}+2C_{\mathrm d})\frac{\mathfrak D(g)}{C} \leq K_{\mathrm{obs}}\mathcal E(g). \label{eq:observable-variance}$$
  - Explanation: Observables that are free on transversals and constant on each defect type have variance bounded by the Dirichlet form, without bounding fluctuations inside a defect type.
- **claim-time-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-time-average)): Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>. Let $G$ be a real function on $\mathcal X$ with $\mathcal QG=G$. Suppose $S_0$ has law $\nu$ satisfying $\nu(S)\leq U\pi(S)$ for every state. For $N\geq1$ consecutive observations of the chain, $$\mathbb E_\nu\left[ \left(\frac1N\sum_{t=0}^{N-1}G(S_t)-\mathbb E_\pi G\right)^2 \right] \leq\frac{2UK_{\mathrm{obs}}}{N}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\pi(G). \label{eq:time-average-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Trajectory averages of controlled observables from a warm start have mean squared error decaying inversely with the number of observations.
- **claim-trace-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace)): Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>. The trace kernel $P_{\mathrm{tr}}$ is irreducible, reversible with stationary law $\mu$, and has holding probability at least $1/2$. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $2p^2C_{\mathrm p}\leq K_{\mathrm{obs}}$. In particular, with $\mu_{\min}=\min_{x\in\mathcal A}\mu(x)>0$, for every integer $s\geq0$, $$\left\|\frac{P_{\mathrm{tr}}^s(x,y)}{\mu(y)}-1\right\| \leq\mu_{\min}^{-1}\exp(-s/K_{\mathrm{obs}}) \qquad(x,y\in\mathcal A). \label{eq:trace-relative-mixing}$$ If the initial trace law satisfies $\nu\leq U\mu$ pointwise, then $$\mathbb E_\nu T_{\mathcal A}^+ \leq\frac{U}{\pi(\mathcal A)}. \label{eq:trace-return-cost}$$ Furthermore, at every deterministic trace-step index the law remains bounded by $U\mu$. The expected total number of underlying transitions for $s$ trace steps is at most $sU/\pi(\mathcal A)$.
  - Explanation: The chain watched only at returns to transversals is irreducible and reversible, with a bounded inverse gap, relative mixing and a bounded expected return cost from warm starts.
- **claim-weight-evaluation** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-weight-oracle)): Fix a box-valid small-count profile $(x,y)$ with total $p$, an integer $0\le j\le H$, and rational parameters $0<\xi_{\mathrm{in}},\theta<1/4$. There is a randomized procedure returning a positive rational $\widehat h$ such that $$\mathbb P\left[ (1-\xi_{\mathrm{in}})h_j(x,y)\le\widehat h \le(1+\xi_{\mathrm{in}})h_j(x,y) \right]\ge1-\theta.$$ On every execution its bit cost is bounded by a fixed polynomial in $L,\varepsilon^{-1},\xi_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1},\theta^{-1}$ and the encoding lengths of the parameters. If $M_f=\prod_{s\ \mathrm{free}}(b_s+1)$, every returned value satisfies $$\frac{M_f2^{-j}}{16}\le\widehat h\le8M_f. \label{eq:oracle-unconditional-range}$$ In particular, positivity and the representation-length bound do not depend on the statistical success event.
  - Explanation: A randomized binned-walk procedure approximates a softened completion weight within the requested relative tolerance, with a polynomial bit cost on every execution and range guarantees independent of the success event.
- **claim-integral-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-integral-interpolation)): Let $S_0,S_1,S_2$ be finite unions of bounded open axis-aligned boxes contained in a common compact box. Let $0<t<1$ and suppose $(1-t)S_1+tS_2\subseteq S_0$. Let $f_0,f_1,f_2$ be positive continuous functions on the ambient compact box such that $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t \qquad(x\in S_1,\ y\in S_2).$$ Then $$\int_{S_0}f_0\ge \left(\int_{S_1}f_1\right)^{1-t} \left(\int_{S_2}f_2\right)^t.$$
  - Explanation: A finite-box form of the Prekopa-Leindler inequality for unions of axis-aligned boxes, proved in one dimension and then by induction on dimension.
- **claim-bin-conductance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bin-conductance)): For $e\ge1$, every nontrivial cut of this chain has stationary flow divided by its smaller stationary side mass at least $$\kappa=\frac{1}{10^4d^2B}.$$
  - Explanation: The local Metropolis walk on the bin grid has a polynomially small lower bound on conductance for every nontrivial cut.
- **claim-dyadic-integer-draw** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-integer-draw)): For a positive integer $M$ and $0<\zeta<1$, choose the smallest nonnegative integer $k$ with $M2^{-k}\le\zeta$. If $Y$ is uniform on $\{0,\ldots,2^k-1\}$, then $\lfloor MY/2^k\rfloor$ has total-variation distance at most $\zeta$ from the uniform law on $\{0,\ldots,M-1\}$. The draw uses exactly $k$ fair bits and bounded integer arithmetic.
  - Explanation: A fixed number of fair bits produces an integer within a prescribed total-variation distance of the uniform law, without any rejection step.
- **claim-initialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-initialization)): For every state of the enlarged binary state space and $0\leq j<H$, $$\frac12\leq\frac{f_{j+1}}{f_j}\leq 1. \label{eq:alg-adjacent}$$ Consequently the ideal multiplier $C(j)/c_{il}(j)$ changes by a factor in $[1/2,2]$ between consecutive parameters, and $\mu_j\leq 2\mu_{j+1}$ pointwise. Moreover, $$\min_{S\in\mathcal A}\mu_j(S) \geq 2^{-(H+p+K)} \qquad (0\leq j\leq H). \label{eq:alg-minimum-mass}$$ The values $C(0)$ and $c_{il}(0)$, and hence all initial ideal multipliers, are computable exactly in polynomial bit time. The law $\mu_0$ has the following finite-choice description: independently for each small cell $s$, choose a uniform integer $k_s\in\{0,\ldots,b_s\}$, and then a uniform $k_s$-element subset of its $b_s$ labeled pairs to use their row elements.
  - Explanation: Adjacent annealing weights differ by a bounded factor, giving a lower bound on transversal masses and exact computability of all initial totals and multipliers, with a simple finite-choice description of the starting law.
- **claim-annealing-accuracy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-annealing)): With probability at least $1-1/50$, the reference experiment does not abort, every stored multiplier collection is good at its own parameter in the sense of <a href="#eq:good-multipliers" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-multipliers">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>, and $$\left\|\log\frac{Z_{\rm ref}}{C(H)}\right\| \leq 4H\eta. \label{eq:alg-reference-error}$$
  - Explanation: With high probability the reference experiment does not abort, every stored multiplier collection is good, and the logarithm of the product estimate is close to that of the final softened count.
- **claim-bounded-implementation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-implementation)): The implemented run uses a number of bit operations bounded, on every execution, by a fixed polynomial in $L$ and $\varepsilon^{-1}$. It returns a nonnegative rational of polynomial bit length. It can be coupled to the capped reference experiment so that, except on an event of probability at most $10J_*\xi$, all state trajectories, type counts, cap decisions, and stored multipliers agree, every requested weight evaluation meets its relative tolerance, and each positive observed term of $G_j$ differs from its reference value by a factor whose logarithm has absolute value at most $4\xi$.
  - Explanation: The fair-bit implementation has a polynomial bit-operation bound on every execution and can be coupled to the capped reference experiment except on a small event.
- **claim-integral-flows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-integral-flows)): For the network data above, there are algorithms with these guarantees.* 1. *Given rational $\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)$, the counting algorithm outputs a nonnegative rational $\widehat Z_{\mathrm{flow}}$ satisfying $$\mathbb P\bigl((1-\varepsilon)\|\mathcal F\|\le\widehat Z_{\mathrm{flow}} \le(1+\varepsilon)\|\mathcal F\|\bigr)\ge1-\delta.$$ If $\mathcal F$ is empty, it outputs zero on every execution.* 2. *Given rational $\tau\in(0,1)$, the sampling algorithm reports infeasibility exactly when $\mathcal F$ is empty. Otherwise it outputs $\widehat f\in\mathcal F$ on every execution, with $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal L(\widehat f),\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal F)\bigr) =\frac12\sum_{f\in\mathcal F} \left\|\mathbb P(\widehat f=f)-\frac1{\|\mathcal F\|}\right\|\le\tau.$$* *Both algorithms use independent unbiased bits. Let $L$ denote the total binary input length, including the corresponding accuracy parameters. Fixed polynomials in $L,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1})$ for counting and in $L,\tau^{-1}$ for sampling bound the total bit operations on every execution.
  - Explanation: Bounded integral flows on explicit directed networks reduce to cell-bounded tables, giving an approximate counter and a total-variation sampler with polynomial cost on every execution.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-binary-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "The addition property, with positivity on the entire valid-degree domain, is preserved by fixing elements included or excluded and by summing a two-element group at occupancy one.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-distinct-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "Even with a constant count weight, the factorial construction need not give $f$ the addition property. For example, take two small cells with bounds $3$ and $2$, with base weight $h\\equiv1$, so the binary ground set has ten elements and $p=5$. Fix as included the three row elements of the first cell. The seven remaining elements are its three column elements and the four elements of the second cell. In the addition matrix of $f$, restrict to vectors with common value $a$ on the first group of three and common value $b$ on the second group of four. Direct substitution in <a href=\"#eq:lifts\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lifts\">[eq:lifts]</a> gives the quadratic form $$6a^2+12ab+8b^2.$$ Its coefficient matrix $\\left(\\begin{smallmatrix}6&6\\\\6&8\\end{smallmatrix}\\right)$ is positive definite, with determinant $12$. Thus this addition matrix has at least two positive eigenvalues. The omission property of $f$ and the addition property of $\\widetilde f$ are the respective assertions needed below.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rem-distinct-lifts",

The seven remaining elements are its three column elements and the four elements of the second cell. In the addition matrix of $f$, restrict to vectors with common value $a$ on the first group of three and common value $b$ on the second group of four. Thus this addition matrix has at least two positive eigenvalues. The omission property of $f$ and the addition property of $\\widetilde f$ are the respective assertions needed below.",
          "source_id": "PM-115.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Fix disjoint sets of included and excluded elements. Let $F$ be the set of fixed inclusions, and partition the remaining elements into two singleton slots $P_0,P_1$ and $s$ ordinary slots of two elements each. Assume $|F|+s+1=p$. Let $\\mathcal X_{\\mathrm{sl}}$ consist of the sets that respect the fixed assignments and select one element from every slot except one hole slot, from which they select nothing. Give a state with hole slot $i$ weight $W_i f(S)$, where every $W_i$ is positive. For a real function $g$ on $\\mathcal X_{\\mathrm{sl}}$, let $D_{\\mathrm{sl}}(g)$ be the unordered-edge energy on its single-exchange graph, with conductance equal to the minimum of the two endpoint weights.*\n\n\n*Let $s_0,s_1$ be the unmultiplied $f$-masses of the states with hole $P_0,P_1$, and let $\\bar g_0,\\bar g_1$ be their respective $f$-weighted means. For each ordinary slot $t$, let $v_t$ be the unmultiplied mass of sets that omit both singleton slots, select both elements of $t$, and select one element from every other ordinary slot, while respecting the fixed assignments. Then $$(\\bar g_0-\\bar g_1)^2\n \\leq\n \\left(\n   \\frac{1}{s_0W_{P_0}}+\\frac{1}{s_1W_{P_1}}\n   +\\frac{2}{s_0s_1}\\sum_t\\frac{v_t}{W_t}\n \\right)D_{\\mathrm{sl}}(g).\n \\label{eq:slot-transport}$$ The notation $W_i$ here denotes slot multipliers, independently of the capacity threshold $W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

For a real function $g$ on $\\mathcal X_{\\mathrm{sl}}$, let $D_{\\mathrm{sl}}(g)$ be the unordered-edge energy on its single-exchange graph, with conductance equal to the minimum of the two endpoint weights.*\n\n\n*Let $s_0,s_1$ be the unmultiplied $f$-masses of the states with hole $P_0,P_1$, and let $\\bar g_0,\\bar g_1$ be their respective $f$-weighted means. For each ordinary slot $t$, let $v_t$ be the unmultiplied mass of sets that omit both singleton slots, select both elements of $t$, and select one element from every other ordinary slot, while respecting the fixed assignments. Then $$(\\bar g_0-\\bar g_1)^2\n \\leq\n \\left(\n   \\frac{1}{s_0W_{P_0}}+\\frac{1}{s_1W_{P_1}}\n   +\\frac{2}{s_0s_1}\\sum_t\\frac{v_t}{W_t}\n \\right)D_{\\mathrm{sl}}(g).\n \\label{eq:slot-transport}$$ The notation $W_i$ here denotes slot multipliers, independently of the capacity threshold $W$.",
          "source_id": "PM-115.2"

      "exact_statement": "Fix disjoint sets of included and excluded elements.         "exact_quote": "Suppose the multipliers satisfy <a href=\"#eq:good-multipliers\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:good-multipliers\">[eq:good-multipliers]</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-weight-oracle",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a box-valid small-count profile $(x,y)$ with total $p$, an integer $0\\le j\\le H$, and rational parameters $0<\\xi_{\\mathrm{in}},\\theta<1/4$.         "exact_quote": "Let $S_0,S_1,S_2$ be finite unions of bounded open axis-aligned boxes contained in a common compact box. Let $0<t<1$ and suppose $(1-t)S_1+tS_2\\subseteq S_0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-integral-flows",
        "exact_quote": "For the network data above, there are algorithms with these guarantees.*\n\n1.  *Given rational $\\varepsilon,\\delta\\in(0,1)$, the counting algorithm outputs a nonnegative rational $\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P\\bigl((1-\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\le\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}\n                 \\le(1+\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\bigr)\\ge1-\\delta.$$ If $\\mathcal F$ is empty, it outputs zero on every execution.*\n\n2.  *Given rational $\\tau\\in(0,1)$, the sampling algorithm reports infeasibility exactly when $\\mathcal F$ is empty. Otherwise it outputs $\\widehat f\\in\\mathcal F$ on every execution, with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\widehat f),\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathcal F)\\bigr)\n     =\\frac12\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F}\n       \\left|\\mathbb P(\\widehat f=f)-\\frac1{|\\mathcal F|}\\right|\\le\\tau.$$*\n\n*Both algorithms use independent unbiased bits. Let $L$ denote the total binary input length, including the corresponding accuracy parameters. Fixed polynomials in $L,\\varepsilon^{-1},\\log(\\delta^{-1})$ for counting and in $L,\\tau^{-1}$ for sampling bound the total bit operations on every execution.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-integral-flows",
          "exact_quote": "For the network data above, there are algorithms with these guarantees.*\n\n1.  *Given rational $\\varepsilon,\\delta\\in(0,1)$, the counting algorithm outputs a nonnegative rational $\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P\\bigl((1-\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\le\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}\n                 \\le(1+\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\bigr)\\ge1-\\delta.$$ If $\\mathcal F$ is empty, it outputs zero on every execution.*\n\n2.  *Given rational $\\tau\\in(0,1)$, the sampling algorithm reports infeasibility exactly when $\\mathcal F$ is empty. Otherwise it outputs $\\widehat f\\in\\mathcal F$ on every execution, with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\widehat f),\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathcal F)\\bigr)\n     =\\frac12\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F}\n       \\left|\\mathbb P(\\widehat f=f)-\\frac1{|\\mathcal F|}\\right|\\le\\tau.$$*\n\n*Both algorithms use independent unbiased bits. Let $L$ denote the total binary input length, including the corresponding accuracy parameters. Fixed polynomials in $L,\\varepsilon^{-1},\\log(\\delta^{-1})$ for counting and in $L,\\tau^{-1}$ for sampling bound the total bit operations on every execution.",
          "source_id": "PM-115.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the network data above, there are algorithms with these guarantees.*\n\n1.  *Given rational $\\varepsilon,\\delta\\in(0,1)$, the counting algorithm outputs a nonnegative rational $\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P\\bigl((1-\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\le\\widehat Z_{\\mathrm{flow}}\n                 \\le(1+\\varepsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\bigr)\\ge1-\\delta.$$ If $\\mathcal F$ is empty, it outputs zero on every execution.*\n\n2.  *Given rational $\\tau\\in(0,1)$, the sampling algorithm reports infeasibility exactly when $\\mathcal F$ is empty. Otherwise it outputs $\\widehat f\\in\\mathcal F$ on every execution, with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\widehat f),\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathcal F)\\bigr)\n     =\\frac12\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F}\n       \\left|\\mathbb P(\\widehat f=f)-\\frac1{|\\mathcal F|}\\right|\\le\\tau.$$*\n\n*Both algorithms use independent unbiased bits. Let $L$ denote the total binary input length, including the corresponding accuracy parameters. Fixed polynomials in $L,\\varepsilon^{-1},\\log(\\delta^{-1})$ for counting and in $L,\\tau^{-1}$ for sampling bound the total bit operations on every execution.",
      "explanation": "Bounded integral flows on explicit directed networks reduce to cell-bounded tables, giving an approximate counter and a total-variation sampler with polynomial cost on every execution.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **cell-bounded contingency table** ([source](#eq:feasible-set)): A cell-bounded contingency table is a nonnegative integer matrix whose row sums, column sums, and individual entry bounds are prescribed.
- **at most one positive eigenvalue** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A symmetric matrix has *at most one positive eigenvalue* when its positive inertia index is at most one, with multiplicity.
- **small and large cells** ([source](#eq:capacity-product)): Call a remaining cell *small* if $b_s<W$, and *large* otherwise.
- **transversal** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *transversal* selects one element from each pair.
- **defect type** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Such a state has *defect type* $(i,l)$ when pair $i$ is empty and pair $l$ is full.
- **balanced pair summation** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Summing such a pair *at balance* means summing its profiles $(k,M_t-k)$, $0\le k\le M_t$.
- **omission property** ([source](#pm-h-17)): The *omission property* is the addition property of the complement-set weight.
- **lift comparison parameter q** ([source](#eq:lift-comparison)): Put $q=W^2$.
- **defect-mean constant** ([source](#pm-h-23)): Set $$C_{\mathrm d}=8+32pq^3.$$

| q-dimension-d | d | = | $mn+m+n+2$ | m,n dimensions |
| q-log-scale-K | K | = | $20\bigl(L+d+\lceil\log_2(1/\varepsilon)\rceil+1\bigr)$ | input length L, accuracy |
| q-boundary-width-gamma | \gamma | = | $\frac{\varepsilon}{512dK}$ | relative width near a tree boundary |
| q-penalty-strength-A | A | = | $\left\lceil\frac{4K}{\gamma}\right\rceil$ | penalty strength |
| q-bins-B | B | = | $16Ad^2$ | bins per free coordinate |
| q-threshold-W | W | = | $100dB$ | separation of small and large capacities |
| q-final-index-H | H | = | $10Ad^2(W+1)$ | penalty bound and final annealing index |
| q-far-mass | 2^{-3K} | ≤ | $\frac{\varepsilon}{128}$ | definition of K |
| q-contamination-upper | C(H) | ≤ | $(1+\varepsilon/32)Z$ | all admissible epsilon |
| q-lift-comparison-q | q | = | $W^2$ | lift comparison |
| q-transversal-variance-constant | C_{\mathrm p} | = | $p(1+8pq^2)$ | transversal variance |
| q-defect-mean-constant | C_{\mathrm d} | = | $8+32pq^3$ | defect means |
| q-observable-constant | K_{\mathrm{obs}} | = | $10p^4(9C_{\mathrm p}+2C_{\mathrm d})$ | observable variance |
| q-weight-oracle-upper | \widehat h | ≤ | $8M_f$ | independent of the success event |
| q-bin-population-upper | \alpha_v | ≤ | $2$ | all bin indices |
| q-bin-conductance | \kappa | ≥ | $\frac{1}{10^4d^2B}$ | all nontrivial cuts |
| q-bin-inverse-gap | K_{\mathrm{bin}} | = | $2(10^4d^2B)^2$ | inverse spectral gap bound |
| q-adjacent-ratio-lower | \frac{f_{j+1}}{f_j} | ≥ | $\frac12$ | every state |
| q-min-transversal-mass | \min_{S\in\mathcal A}\mu_j(S) | ≥ | $2^{-(H+p+K)}$ | all annealing parameters |
| q-tolerance-eta | \eta | = | $\frac{\varepsilon}{200(H+1)}$ | per-phase relative tolerance |
| q-restart-budget | B_{\rm ret} | = | $2000p^2(H+1)^2\tau$ | restart transition budget |
| q-reference-log-error | \left\|\log\frac{Z_{\rm ref}}{C(H)}\right\| | ≤ | $4H\eta$ | success event |
| q-implementation-log-error | \left\|\log\frac{\widehat Z}{C(H)}\right\| | ≤ | $\frac{\varepsilon}{16}$ | constant-success event |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-forest** [claim](#stmt-lem-forest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forest): Exchange argument on a maximum-capacity forest, leaf elimination, and cut identities for displacement bounds. -> Unique integral tree completion with bounded displacement, penalty and Lipschitz estimate.
- **step-integer-signature** [claim](#stmt-lem-integer-signature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integer-signature): Laminar rank-one identity, then induction over summed pairs with cumulative-sum telescoping, then reflection for additions. -> Two-removal and two-addition matrices with at most one positive eigenvalue after balanced summation.
- **step-hj-signatures** [claim](#stmt-cor-hj-signatures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hj-signatures): Represent the softened weight as a laminar model on physical intervals and take the equality limit. -> Signature property for the completion weights.
- **step-tree-marginal** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-marginal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-marginal): Sum all other pairs at balance, read off a two-by-two determinant, then pass to the limit. -> Log-concave tree marginals with interval support.
- **step-contamination** [claim](#stmt-prop-contamination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-marginal-2): Far assignments are exponentially penalized; near violations are bounded by interpolating log-concave marginals toward attainable endpoints. -> Sandwich bound between the true count and the softened count.
- **step-binary-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-contraction): Cubic tensor projection with a positive eigenvector and a stochastic-matrix argument. -> Closure of addition and omission properties under fixing and pair summation.
- **step-binary-signatures** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-signatures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-signatures): Group decomposition of the quadratic form with multiplicity cancellation, reduced to the completion-weight signatures. -> Omission property of the first lift and addition property of the second.
- **step-distinct-lifts-example** [claim](#stmt-rem-distinct-lifts) / [proof](#stmt-rem-distinct-lifts): Direct substitution gives an explicit positive definite two-by-two form. -> The first lift lacks the addition property in general.
- **step-defect-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-lem-defect-inequalities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-defect-inequalities): Determinant of a restricted two-by-two matrix and a Schur complement on a contracted three-by-three matrix. -> Pair and triple defect-mass inequalities.
- **step-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport): Recursive balanced demands, clique routing and Schur complement bounds on potential increments. -> Bound on squared difference of weighted means by Dirichlet energy.
- **step-transversal-variance** [claim](#stmt-prop-transversal-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transversal-variance): Conditioning tree with sink choice, transport at each node, and disjoint edge sets per depth. -> Transversal variance bound by the Dirichlet form.
- **step-defect-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-defect-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-defect-mean): One-hole transport between a defect type and a conditional transversal class, plus conditional Cauchy-Schwarz. -> Defect means near the transversal mean.
- **step-observable-variance** [claim](#stmt-prop-observable-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-observable-variance): Decompose variance of the projected observable into transversal and defect parts. -> Restricted variance bound by the Dirichlet form.
- **step-time-average** [claim](#stmt-lem-time-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-time-average): Resolvent bound from the observable inequality, covariance sum, and domination of the initial law. -> Mean squared error of trajectory averages.
- **step-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace): Harmonic extension identity, spectral contraction, and occupation measure for the return cost. -> Trace gap, relative mixing and return cost.
- **step-integral-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-integral-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integral-interpolation): Quantile coupling in one dimension, then induction on dimension through slices. -> Integral interpolation inequality.
- **step-bin-conductance** [claim](#stmt-lem-bin-conductance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bin-conductance): Expansion of the smaller side by interpolation, covering by cut-face neighborhoods, comparison of density and bin weights. -> Polynomial lower bound on bin-chain conductance.
- **step-dyadic-draw** [claim](#stmt-lem-dyadic-integer-draw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dyadic-integer-draw): Count preimages of each output of a scaled floor map. -> Near-uniform integer draw with fixed bit usage.
- **step-weight-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-prop-weight-oracle) / [proof](#pm-h-31): Binned estimator with telescoping ratios, population correction, bin-chain mixing, dyadic draws and rational rounding. -> Randomized weight estimate with unconditional range and bit bounds.
- **step-initialization** [claim](#stmt-lem-initialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initialization): Termwise comparison of adjacent weights and explicit factorial formulas at the initial parameter. -> Adjacent ratio bounds, minimum mass and exact initial multipliers.
- **step-annealing** [claim](#stmt-prop-annealing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-annealing): Phase-by-phase induction with a virtual uncapped restart, Markov and union bounds, and multiplier updates from type counts. -> Reference accuracy with high probability.
- **step-bounded-implementation** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-implementation): Counting of random actions, coupling with common uniforms, and size bounds on stored multipliers and outputs. -> Coupling to the reference and a polynomial bound on every execution.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-implementation-2): Combine contamination, reference and implementation bounds, treat the zero-profile case, then amplify by a median. -> The approximation scheme with all-execution polynomial cost.
- **step-integral-flows** [claim](#stmt-cor-integral-flows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-integral-flows): Subdivide arcs and build a bijective table, then sample by bisecting cell intervals with approximate conditional counts. -> Flow counter and total-variation sampler.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-initial-multiplier-closed-form**: At annealing parameter zero, for distinct pair labels i and l, the ideal defect multiplier C(0)/c_il(0) equals 6 when both pairs lie in the same small cell, and 6(b_{s(i)}+1)/(b_{s(l)}+2) when they lie in different small cells. This is the finite arithmetic identity behind the exact initialization of the multipliers.
- **inv-initial-multipliers-exact**: The initial totals C(0) and c_il(0), and hence all initial ideal multipliers, are computable exactly by finite arithmetic. Brute-force enumeration of p-subsets must agree with the closed form.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-exact-multipliers-later-phases**: Reuse the closed-form initial multipliers at later annealing parameters instead of learning them from observed type frequencies. -> The closed form is valid only at parameter zero, where the weight h_0 is constant; at later parameters the defect totals depend on the penalty and are not given by it.
- **anti-checker-as-fpras**: Treat the exact brute-force subset enumeration used to check the multiplier formula as the counting algorithm for tables. -> Subset enumeration is exponential in p; the paper's guarantee comes from a randomized scheme with polynomial bit cost, and the proof-side constructions are not built by the algorithm.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The exact initialization of the defect multipliers at parameter zero is a finite arithmetic construction with an explicit closed form, so it can be executed and checked against brute-force enumeration on small instances. The randomized annealing and Markov-chain parts are not finite constructive results and are not tested here.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin line 1 = small-cell bounds b_1..b_k (positive integers, space separated); line 2 = two distinct pair labels i l in 1..p where p = sum of bounds, pairs numbered cell by cell.
# PRECONDITIONS: all bounds >= 1; 1 <= i, l <= p; i != l; if i and l are in the same cell its bound is >= 2.
# OUTPUTS: one line n/d, the reduced fraction w_il^(0) = C(0)/c_il(0).
# TIME: O(k) arithmetic operations on small integers.
# SPACE: O(k) for the bound list.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Python integers and Fraction; no floating point.
# FAILURE: raises ValueError (nonzero exit) if a pair label is out of range.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def cell_of(pair, bs):
    acc = 0
    for k, b in enumerate(bs):
        acc += b
        if pair <= acc:
            return k
    raise ValueError('pair label out of range')

def main():
    lines = sys.stdin.read().splitlines()
    bs = [int(t) for t in lines[0].split()]
    i, l = [int(t) for t in lines[1].split()]
    ci = cell_of(i, bs)
    cl = cell_of(l, bs)
    if ci == cl:
        num, den = 6, 1
    else:
        num, den = 6 * (bs[ci] + 1), bs[cl] + 2
    r = Fraction(num, den)
    print(str(r.numerator) + '/' + str(r.denominator))

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives a uniform complexity guarantee rather than a practical running-time estimate, so it does not ship a ready library routine; its polynomial bounds are deliberately generous. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No game, level generation or interactive simulation setting appears; the only sampling is a total-variation sampler of feasible flows. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives no tool or command-line workflow; its objects are proof-internal chains and weights that the algorithm never constructs. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Theorem 1 is a randomized counting algorithm for cell-bounded tables with all-execution polynomial bit cost, built from tightening, an annealing chain and median amplification of independent runs. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure with operations or invariants is proposed; the spanning forest and state sets serve only the analysis and the algorithm's bookkeeping. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper addresses parallel or hardware execution; the cost model is sequential bit operations. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The unbiased bits are used only for sampling and approximation, with no security notion, adversary or hardness assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem is posed; the multipliers are learned from observed type frequencies only inside the annealing estimator. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Feasibility and tightening use a maximum-flow network with binary search on cell extrema, and a maximum-capacity spanning forest organises the large cells; counting feasible integral flows is the resulting operations-research use. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no message passing, replication or concurrency model; its networks are static flow instances. |
| other (Statistical counting and almost-uniform sampling of integral flows) | True | moderate | adaptable | stated | Corollary 24 turns the table result into an FPRAS for integral flows with bounds and balances and a total-variation sampler that always returns a feasible vector when one exists. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-feasible-input-and-binary-dimensions** (hypothesis): The counting guarantee concerns tables whose margins have equal totals, with both dimensions part of the input and all data in binary. It says nothing about unary-encoded inputs or about instances whose data are not given explicitly. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to inputs where the margins do not have equal totals', 'Reading the polynomial cost as polynomial in the numerical size of the capacities']
- **limit-infeasible-returns-zero** (edge_case): Infeasible instances are detected by a deterministic flow test and return zero. The approximation scheme itself runs only on feasible, tightened instances. Blocked use: ['Using a zero estimate from the randomized phase as a certificate of infeasibility', 'Skipping the feasibility test before tightening cell bounds']
- **limit-uniform-not-practical** (formal_scope): The result is a uniform complexity guarantee. Its polynomial bounds are deliberately generous and are not practical running-time estimates. Blocked use: ["Using the proof's parameter values as production tuning constants", 'Predicting wall-clock cost of an implementation from the stated polynomials']
- **limit-proof-only-objects** (nonconstructive): The large matrices, conditioning trees and transport flows serve only the analysis. The algorithm never builds them, so they cannot be reused as computational objects. Blocked use: ['Implementing the conditioning tree or the transport flow as a routine', 'Treating the signature matrices as quantities the algorithm computes']
- **limit-lift-roles-distinct** (counterexample_scope): The first lift has the omission property but need not have the addition property, and the second plays the opposite role. Substituting one lift for the other in the signature arguments is invalid. Blocked use: ['Applying the defect-mass inequalities to the first lift', 'Applying the one-hole transport argument to the second lift']
- **limit-weight-oracle-success-event** (approximation): The weight estimate is accurate only with the stated probability. On the failure event the returned value is still positive and within the stated range, but it carries no accuracy guarantee. Blocked use: ['Assuming every individual weight call meets its relative tolerance', 'Using a single evaluation as an exact value of the completion weight']
- **limit-flow-balances-and-sampler** (hypothesis): The flow corollary needs bounds with lower at most upper and balances summing to zero; otherwise the flow set is empty. The sampler is a total-variation approximation with cost polynomial in the inverse error, not an exact sampler. Blocked use: ['Counting flows with unmodelled drops where the balances do not sum to zero', 'Using the sampler as exact, or assuming cost polynomial in the logarithm of the inverse error']

>
> - [Exact Uniform Sampling of Contingency Tables with Arbitrary Margins](../../preprints/Exact-Uniform-Sampling-of-Contingency-Tables-with-Arbitrary-Margins-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [An FPRAS for Cell-Bounded Contingency Tables](../../preprints/An-FPRAS-for-Cell-Bounded-Contingency-Tables-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives an exact uniform sampler for nonnegative integer contingency tables with arbitrary prescribed row and column sums having equal totals. It terminates almost surely, every halted output is feasible, and each table has exactly the uniform limiting probability. Expected bit complexity is polynomial in the dimensions and the binary length of the margins.
>
> It also gives a sampler with a fixed polynomial time bound on every execution whose total-variation error is at most $2^{-k}$ for requested precision $k\ge1$. The same Comparator file includes the companion approximation scheme for counting tables with individual cell bounds.
>
> The formalization gives a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting nonnegative integer matrices with prescribed row sums, column sums, and individual entry bounds. Both dimensions may vary, the margins and bounds are binary encoded, and zero entry bounds are allowed. For rational $0<\varepsilon,\delta<1$, the algorithm returns a nonnegative estimate with relative error at most $\varepsilon$ with probability at least $1-\delta$, and returns zero on every execution when no table exists.
>
> The running time is polynomial in the encoded input size, $\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\log(\delta^{-1})$ on every random tape. The same Comparator file also contains the companion sampling results for tables without cell bounds.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace MatchingFPRAS

abbrev Symbol := Fin 8

/-- Binary numeral followed by a delimiter. Tape blank is 0; bits use 1 and 2. -/
def encodeNat (n : ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  (Nat.bits n).map (fun b => if b then 2 else 1) ++ [3]

def encodeInt (z : ℤ) : List Symbol :=
  (if z < 0 then [5] else [4]) ++ encodeNat z.natAbs

/-- A rational encoded by its reduced numerator and positive denominator. -/
def encodeRat (q : ℚ) : List Symbol := encodeInt q.num ++ encodeNat q.den

/-- A uniform randomized Post-Turing machine: a single finite transition table. -/
structure RandomMachine where
  states : ℕ
  transition : Fin (states + 1) → Symbol → Bool →
    Option (Fin (states + 1) × Turing.TM0.Stmt Symbol)

structure Configuration (A : RandomMachine) where
  state : Option (Fin (A.states + 1))
  tape : Turing.Tape Symbol

/-- A halted configuration is absorbing. Each nonhalting tick is exactly one
write or one move, or the detection of halt. -/
def tick (A : RandomMachine) (c : Configuration A) (bit : Bool) : Configuration A :=
  match c.state with
  | none => c
  | some s => match A.transition s c.tape.head bit with
    | none => ⟨none, c.tape⟩
    | some (s', action) =>
      ⟨some s', match action with
        | .move d => c.tape.move d
        | .write a => c.tape.write a⟩

def initial (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) : Configuration A :=
  ⟨some 0, Turing.Tape.mk₁ input⟩

/-- Bounded execution on a finite prefix of the random tape. -/
def run (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) {t : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) : Configuration A :=
  (List.ofFn bits).foldl (tick A) (initial A input)

def Outputs (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) {t : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) (q : ℚ) : Prop :=
  (run A input bits).state = none ∧
    (run A input bits).tape.right₀ = Turing.ListBlank.mk (encodeRat q)

end MatchingFPRAS

namespace ContingencyTables

open Finset
variable {I J : Type*} [Fintype I] [Fintype J]

def HasMargins (X : I → J → ℕ) (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, ∑ j, X i j = r i) ∧ (∀ j, ∑ i, X i j = c j)

def Table (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) := {X : I → J → ℕ // HasMargins X r c}

def BoundedTable (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) (b : I → J → ℕ) :=
  {X : Table r c // ∀ i j, X.val i j ≤ b i j}

theorem entry_le_row {X : I → J → ℕ} {r : I → ℕ} {c : J → ℕ}
    (h : HasMargins X r c) (i : I) (j : J) : X i j ≤ r i := by
  rw [← h.1 i]
  exact single_le_sum (fun _ _ => Nat.zero_le _) (mem_univ j)

theorem entry_le_total {X : I → J → ℕ} {r : I → ℕ} {c : J → ℕ}
    (h : HasMargins X r c) (i : I) (j : J) : X i j ≤ ∑ i, r i :=
  (entry_le_row h i j).trans (single_le_sum (fun _ _ => Nat.zero_le _) (mem_univ i))

instance table_finite (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Finite (Table r c) := by
  let encode : Table r c → I → J → Fin ((∑ i, r i) + 1) :=
    fun X i j => ⟨X.val i j, Nat.lt_succ_of_le (entry_le_total X.property i j)⟩
  apply Finite.of_injective encode
  intro X Y h
  apply Subtype.ext
  funext i j
  exact congrArg Fin.val (congrFun (congrFun h i) j)

noncomputable instance table_fintype (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Fintype (Table r c) :=
  Fintype.ofFinite _

noncomputable instance boundedTable_fintype (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) (b : I → J → ℕ) :
    Fintype (BoundedTable r c b) := by
  classical
  unfold BoundedTable
  infer_instance

end ContingencyTables

namespace ContingencyTables.Algorithms

open scoped BigOperators
open Filter

abbrev Machine := MatchingFPRAS.RandomMachine
abbrev Symbol := MatchingFPRAS.Symbol

def encodeMatrix {m n : ℕ} (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  ((List.ofFn fun i => (List.ofFn fun j => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (X i j)).flatten)).flatten

def encodeMargins {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat m ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat n ++
    (List.ofFn fun i => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (r i)).flatten ++
    (List.ofFn fun j => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (c j)).flatten

def encodeSamplingInput {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (k : ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  encodeMargins r c ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat k

def encodeCountingInput {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : List Symbol :=
  encodeMargins r c ++ encodeMatrix b ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeRat ε ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeRat δ

/-- The tape alphabet has positive cardinality. -/
theorem alphabetSize_neZero : NeZero (8 : ℕ) := inferInstance

/-- A halted tape contains the row-major binary matrix encoding. -/
def OutputsTable (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) {t m n : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none ∧
    @Turing.Tape.right₀ MatchingFPRAS.Symbol (@Fin.instInhabited 8 alphabetSize_neZero)
      (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).tape =
    @Turing.ListBlank.mk Symbol (@Fin.instInhabited 8 alphabetSize_neZero) (encodeMatrix X)

noncomputable def tableMass (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) (t : ℕ)
    {m n : ℕ} (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.univ.filter fun bits : Fin t → Bool => OutputsTable A input bits X).card : ℝ) /
    (2 : ℝ) ^ t

noncomputable def haltMass (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) (t : ℕ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.univ.filter fun bits : Fin t → Bool =>
      (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none).card : ℝ) / (2 : ℝ) ^ t

def samplingSize (n : ℕ) {m : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  m + n + Nat.clog 2 ((∑ i, r i) + 1) + k + 1

/-- Paper 200-01, Theorem main(i): bounded runtime on every random tape
and total variation at most `2⁻ᵏ`, with freely varying dimensions. -/
def BoundedSamplingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j → ∀ k : ℕ, 1 ≤ k →
        let input := encodeSamplingInput r c k
        let t := C * (samplingSize n r k) ^ d
        (∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, ∃ X : Table r c, OutputsTable A input bits X.val) ∧
        (∑ X : Table r c,
          |tableMass A input t X.val - (Fintype.card (Table r c) : ℝ)⁻¹|) / 2 ≤
            ((2 : ℝ) ^ k)⁻¹

/-- Paper 200-01, Theorem main(ii): every halted output is feasible, each
table has limiting mass `1/|Ω|`, termination has probability one, and the
expected number of bit operations is bounded by a uniform polynomial. -/
def ExactSamplingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j →
        let input := encodeMargins r c
        let tail : ℕ → ℝ := fun t => 1 - haltMass A input t
        (∀ t (bits : Fin t → Bool), (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none →
          ∃ X : Table r c, OutputsTable A input bits X.val) ∧
        (∀ X : Table r c, Tendsto (fun t => tableMass A input t X.val) atTop
          (nhds ((Fintype.card (Table r c) : ℝ)⁻¹))) ∧
        Tendsto (haltMass A input) atTop (nhds 1) ∧
        Summable tail ∧
        tsum tail ≤ C * (samplingSize n r 0) ^ d

noncomputable def count {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : ℕ := Fintype.card (BoundedTable r c b)

def countingTime (C d : ℕ) {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : ℕ :=
  C * ((encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ).length + ⌈ε⁻¹⌉₊ +
    Nat.clog 2 ⌈δ⁻¹⌉₊ + 1) ^ d

noncomputable def goodCountingTapes (A : Machine) {m n : ℕ}
    (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ)
    (ε δ : ℚ) (t : ℕ) : Finset (Fin t → Bool) := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.filter fun bits => ∃ q : ℚ,
    MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A (encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ) bits q ∧
      (1 - ε) * count r c b ≤ q ∧ q ≤ (1 + ε) * count r c b

/-- Paper 200-02, Theorem main. The input length includes the rational
accuracy parameters, and infeasible inputs always produce exactly zero. -/
def CountingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ), 0 < m → 0 < n →
    ∀ (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j →
      ∀ ε δ : ℚ, 0 < ε → ε < 1 → 0 < δ → δ < 1 →
        let input := encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ
        let t := countingTime C d r c b ε δ
        (∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, ∃ q : ℚ, 0 ≤ q ∧ MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A input bits q) ∧
        (count r c b = 0 → ∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A input bits 0) ∧
        ((goodCountingTapes A r c b ε δ t).card : ℚ) / (2 : ℚ) ^ t ≥ 1 - δ

end ContingencyTables.Algorithms

namespace ContingencyTables

/-- An almost-uniform sampler with polynomial cost on every execution. -/
theorem boundedSampling : Algorithms.BoundedSamplingStatement := by sorry

/-- Exact uniform sampling in polynomial expected bit time. -/
theorem exactSampling : Algorithms.ExactSamplingStatement := by sorry

/-- A cell-bounded FPRAS, with zero output on every infeasible execution. -/
theorem counting : Algorithms.CountingStatement := by sorry

end ContingencyTables

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting nonnegative integer matrices with prescribed row sums, column sums, and individual entry bounds. Both dimensions vary, all numerical data are encoded in binary, and zero bounds are allowed. The algorithm uses only unbiased random bits and has a polynomial bound on its bit operations on every execution.
